interface) plus a new rule: a booking whose payment is PROCESSING is protected from expiry for up
to PAYMENT_PAUSE_MAX_MINUTES (5), via a new PaymentActivity.prepareExpiry() the sweep/expireIfDue

that went with it) since applyStatus now owns the release; added findExpiredHolds's afterId cursor
and used it in the sweep so a skipped or endlessly-failing booking can never be re-fetched forever
or block bookings further down the list; added PaymentActivity (payment-activity.ts) with a stub
that always returns EXPIRE (no Stripe yet - PAY-01 provides the real one) wired into PaymentsModule
behind the PAYMENT_ACTIVITY token; added a shared BookingProvider conformance test
(booking-provider.conformance.ts) that Flights B / Hotels B can run against their real
implementations, self-tested here against a FakeBookingProvider.

PAY-01's actual logic, which doesn't exist yet. The new tests here drive BookingLifecycle through
each of PaymentActivity's three possible answers (SKIP/PAID/EXPIRE) via a configurable fake, which
tests my code's reaction to that signal correctly, but is not a real test of Stripe's timing -
I said so plainly in the PR/Jira reply rather than implying it's been tested end to end.

Date: 2026-11-04
Student: Abdelrahman ElGabarty
Tool: Claude (Claude Code, Sonnet 5)
Related Requirement: GEN-01 (SCRUM-32)
Prompt:
SCRUM-15 merged to develop. Asked to rebase the SCRUM-32 branch onto the new develop, delete the temporary
current-user.stub.ts, wire the real @CurrentUser() from common/, and confirm nothing else needs to change now
that the real JwtAuthGuard/RolesGuard are global. Original draft PR (barely any real work in it, opened only
to track the ticket while blocked on SCRUM-15) was closed; this is a clean PR off current develop instead.

How the output was used:
Rebuilt feature/GEN-01-notifications off current origin/develop. Deleted current-user.stub.ts and changed
notifications.controller.ts's one import to CurrentUser from '../common'. The controller needs no @Roles()
(any logged-in role can have notifications, per the ticket).

Ran the real build/lint/test this time instead of assuming: tsc --noEmit and eslint were clean, but
notifications.controller.spec.ts's three "without a logged-in user -> 401" tests started failing. Cause: that
test builds an isolated Nest testing module with only NotificationsController, so the real global JwtAuthGuard
(registered via APP_GUARD in AuthModule) is never present there - the test's own fake-auth middleware just set
req.user from a header and otherwise did nothing. The old stub decorator used to throw UnauthorizedException
itself when req.user was missing, which accidentally covered for that; the real CurrentUser() decorator just
returns request.user as-is and relies entirely on the guard, so removing the stub removed that accidental
safety net from the test. Fixed by making the test's fake-auth middleware reject with the same
UnauthorizedException('Authentication required') the real guard throws when there is no header/cookie, so the
test now simulates the guard instead of depending on the decorator to self-enforce. Production is unaffected
(AuthModule's APP_GUARD is global across the real app); this was a test-isolation gap, not a runtime bug.
All 43 notifications tests pass after the fix; full build and lint clean.

Student: Ahmed Sanad
Tool: Claude (Claude Code, Sonnet 5.5)
Related Requirement: Auth hardening (follow-up to ACC-04 / SCRUM-15; related to ACC-06 / SCRUM-38 and suspension in ACC-13..16)
Prompt:
A live test showed a suspended user's existing access cookie still returned 200 on every endpoint except /auth/me for up to 15 minutes, because JwtAuthGuard only trusted the signed token. The assistant described three options (accept and document, check the account status on every request, shorten the token); I answered "go with the 2nd option" and then "Yes, build it".
How the output was used:
JwtAuthGuard now asks an injected AccountStatusChecker (ACCOUNT_STATUS_CHECKER token in common/guards, provided in AuthModule by UsersService.isActive) whether the account is still ACTIVE, one status-only lookup by _id per request. Suspended, deactivated, deleted or malformed-id accounts get 401; a failed lookup fails closed (500). Role, airlineId and hotelId still come from the token until the next login or refresh. Public routes do not hit the database. The matching contract line is in the contracts v1.3 PR, not in this one.
I ran clean npm ci, build, lint, 243 unit tests and 81 e2e tests (new tests: guard unit tests, UsersService.isActive, and e2e for suspended, deactivated, reactivated, deleted-user and suspended-admin cases) and repeated the live suspended-cookie check against the rebuilt app.

Related Requirement: ITN-08 (SCRUM-70)
Prompt:
Start working on all my itinerary tickets that are not blocked, one ticket per branch and PR. For ITN-08,
following docs/contracts.md and the Jira checklist: PATCH /api/itineraries/:id/days/reorder moves a day to a new
position, days are renumbered and dates and city groups are recalculated, an invalid target position is rejected,
owner only. Write the tests first, then the code, then open the PR and update Jira.
How the output was used:
Used the design from ITN-05 (number, date and city groups are derived from the position), so a move only changes
the order of the stored days. Used a body of { dayId, position } and one atomic aggregation-pipeline update that
rebuilds the array from what is stored, instead of read, reorder in memory and write back, so simultaneous moves
cannot lose or duplicate a day (tested with 4 at once). Used the route declared before days/:dayId, because
"reorder" would otherwise be read as a day id. A position after the last day is 400, a stranger or unknown day is

rows were left behind); fixed and kept as a test. The public view now builds the budget summary from the rows it already
loads. Transport costs are copied too, although the ticket lists only days, activities and budgets, so the clone shows
the same totals. A mutation check (wrong day id in the budget copy) failed two tests as expected. Lint, build, 880 unit
and 1027 e2e tests pass. Known limits, stated in the PR: not atomic if the server dies mid-clone, cloning a clone credits
the clone's author, the credit shows the original author's name even if that account is later deactivated, and there is
no per-user cap on clones.

Built a new `fx` module (backend/src/fx/): `ExchangeRate` schema (one doc per base+rateDate, Map-typed rates column, unique index), an `ExchangeRateProvider` interface/DI-token pattern mirroring `PaymentActivity`/`PAYMENT_ACTIVITY` with a real `HttpExchangeRateProvider` using Node's global `fetch` against `https://api.fxratesapi.com/latest` (the first outbound HTTP call anywhere in this codebase — verified the real endpoint shape via web search since nobody had integrated FXRatesAPI here before), `FxService.convert`/`ensureTodaysRates` (one shared fetch-and-cache path used by both the lazy check and a new daily `@Cron` job, with stale-cache fallback and a `ServiceUnavailableException` only when nothing has ever been cached), and `FxModule`. Rejected a design-pass proposal to convert every `byCategory`/`byDay` cell of the itinerary budget summary into the target currency (would multiply FX/DB calls by up to `currencies × (1 + 5 categories + 60 days)` per request); instead added one `convertedTotal` grand total alongside the existing per-currency `totals` breakdown, converting once per distinct source currency. Wired `FxModule` into `AppModule` and `ItinerariesModule`; extended `BudgetsService.summary` with an optional `currency` param (falls back to the caller's ACC-09 preferred currency, then the platform currency) and a new `?currency=` query param on `GET /api/itineraries/:id/budget-summary`. Added `FX_API_KEY?: string` to `EnvVars` as optional (no fail-fast check) since FXRatesAPI has a usable free tier and the real `.env` in this environment couldn't safely be read to confirm the key was set (blocked by a safety classifier).
While testing, found that Mongoose's `lean()` flattens a `Map`-typed field to a plain object by default — the schema class itself stays `Map<string, number>`, but `ExchangeRateLean.rates` had to be typed and accessed as `Record<string, number>` instead; fixed `FxService` and the test fakes to match. Added `fx/fx.service.spec.ts` (hand-written fake provider + fake Mongoose model, no real network) and extended `itineraries/budgets.service.spec.ts` with a `FixedExchangeRateProvider` override (via `overrideProvider(EXCHANGE_RATE_PROVIDER)`) so the existing real-MongoDB integration suite never touches the real FXRatesAPI, plus new cases for the default/override/unsupported-currency/no-cost paths.
Verification: `tsc --noEmit` and `tsc -p tsconfig.build.json` clean. `eslint` clean on every new/changed file. `fx.service.spec.ts` (6/6) and `budgets.service.spec.ts` (28/28, including 5 new ITN-18 cases) pass; the full backend Jest suite was also run to confirm no regressions elsewhere. The `sharp` native dependency was missing from this environment's `node_modules` (installed with `npm install sharp --no-save` purely to run the pre-existing itineraries/uploads test suites locally — not committed, unrelated to this ticket's own code). No `.env`, secrets, commit, push, branch switch, or Jira change happened as part of this implementation pass itself.

Prompt:
Implement PAY-03 against docs/contracts.md and the authoritative reviewed-requirements sheet: for every cancellation with a
positive refund amount (user cancellations per the provider's tiers; provider-initiated at 100%), issue a refund through the
Stripe Refunds API, record it, and show its status (Pending/Refunded/Failed) on the booking details, notifying the user. Expose
RefundsService.issue({bookingType, bookingId, amountCents, reason}) for FLT-26/HTL-21/FLT-06 (owned by other devs) to call from
their own cancel endpoints — do not build those endpoints myself, only publish the contract. Dependencies (PAY-01, the shared

same owner), with its own `Refund` schema (bookingType/bookingId/userId/stripePaymentIntentId/stripeRefundId/amountCents/
currency/status/reason, a unique index on stripePaymentIntentId so a booking's payment is ever refunded exactly once). Added
`StripeGateway.createRefund` (+ fake) alongside the existing PAY-01 methods, storing the free-text reason in Stripe metadata
rather than its own constrained `reason` enum. `RefundsService.issue` validates the amount is a positive integer (rejecting
before any Stripe call, satisfying "zero refund creates no Stripe call"), looks up the booking's SUCCEEDED Payment to enforce

pattern), calls Stripe, records PENDING/REFUNDED/FAILED from the synchronous response, and notifies via NotificationsService.
A refund left PENDING settles later through a new `refund.updated` webhook case added to PaymentsService.handleWebhookEvent,
delegating to RefundsService.applyStripeRefundEvent (idempotent: only updates a still-PENDING row, notifies only on an actual
change). Replaced the existing TODO(PAY-03) in PaymentsService.onSucceeded (the paid-but-already-expired edge case from
contracts.md section 8) with a real call into RefundsService.issue. To avoid a module import cycle (PaymentsModule needs
RefundsService; RefundsModule needs the Payment schema), RefundsModule registers its own MongooseModule.forFeature binding for
the Payment schema class (reused, read-only) instead of importing PaymentsModule. Published `issue` and a second method,
`getLatestForBooking(bookingType, bookingId)`, for Flights/Hotels to show refund status on their own booking-details responses
(their files, not mine) — both documented in docs/contracts.md section 7 with the final signatures.
Verification: tsc --noEmit and eslint clean. New refunds.service.spec.ts (hand-rolled fakes, no real DB): zero/negative amount
rejected pre-Stripe-call, over-paid rejected, duplicate-refund-for-one-payment rejected (both the pre-check and the unique-index
race path), REFUNDED-immediately and left-PENDING-then-settled-via-webhook paths, webhook idempotency (no duplicate notify).
Extended payments.service.spec.ts's already-expired-booking test to assert RefundsService.issue is actually called. New
refunds.module.spec.ts and an updated payments.module.spec.ts (overriding the new Refund/Notification model tokens) for DI
wiring. Scoped run (src/refunds, src/payments) and the two related e2e specs (payments-webhook, payments-concurrency) all pass.

Date: 2026-11-18
Student: Abdelrahman ElGabarty
Tool: Claude (Claude Code, Sonnet 5)
Related Requirement: none (GitHub issue #114, found by an audit of develop)
Prompt:
Fix GitHub issue #114: (1) retrievePaymentIntent in stripe-payment-activity.ts has no try/catch, so a Stripe
error during the hold-expiry sweep/lazy check escapes prepareExpiry, crashes a booking read with a 500, and
leaves the hold stuck (the sweep keeps failing the same way every minute); a Stripe failure here should leave
the booking as it is (SKIP, retried next sweep). (2) create/retry in payments.service.ts let a Stripe error from
createPaymentIntent escape as a generic 500; expected a clear 502/503. Fix with tests.
How the output was used:
Wrapped the `retrievePaymentIntent` call in `StripePaymentActivity.prepareExpiry` in a try/catch that returns
'SKIP' on failure, leaving the in-flight payment row untouched so the next sweep or lazy check tries again,
instead of letting the error reach the caller (a booking read) as an unhandled 500. Added a private
`createPaymentIntentOrThrow` helper in `PaymentsService` that wraps the existing `stripe.createPaymentIntent`
call, logs the failure, and throws `ServiceUnavailableException` (503) with a clear "try again shortly" message;
both `create()` and `retry()` now call it instead of the gateway method directly. Added `@ApiServiceUnavailableResponse`
to both endpoints in `payments.controller.ts`. Chose 503 (not 502) since the backend itself is healthy — it is the
upstream payment provider that is unavailable, which is exactly what 503 communicates. Tests use the existing
hand-rolled `FakeStripeGateway`, monkey-patching its `retrievePaymentIntent`/`createPaymentIntent` methods to
reject for one call (the project's established style for a single-shot failure, same as `forceDuplicateKeyOnce`
elsewhere in this module) rather than new e2e specs — these are deterministic, synchronous-failure-mode bugs,
not concurrency, so a real in-memory Mongo/HTTP round trip buys nothing a unit test with a fake doesn't already
prove, and matches how every other Stripe-gateway failure mode in this module is already tested.
Verification: tsc --noEmit and eslint clean. New tests: `stripe-payment-activity.spec.ts` (SKIP on an
unreachable Stripe, in-flight row left untouched) and two in `payments.service.spec.ts` (create/retry each
return ServiceUnavailableException when Stripe is down). Scoped unit (`src/payments`, `src/refunds`): 146/146
pass. Related e2e (`payments-webhook`, `payments-concurrency`, capped at 2 workers): 5/5 pass.

HotelBookingCancellationService (own file, not added to the big HotelBookingsService) and its controller: GET :id/cancel-preview and
POST :id/cancel. The owner filter is part of the query (404 for someone else's, unknown or malformed ids); only CONFIRMED before the check-in
moment (check-in date at the hotel check-in time, UTC, 00:00 when unset) is cancellable, else 409. The refund is RefundCalculator.compute on the
hotel's tiers, the fractional days to check-in and the full total, so preview and cancel agree. The status change is BookingLifecycle.transition
(atomic, frees the rooms in the same write), then RefundsService.issue; a refund of 0 is not issued and refund is null. HotelsModule imports
RefundsModule (one line, flagged in the PR); no other module was edited. Mutation checks: removing the owner filter failed the ownership test, and
disabling the status guard failed five tests.
Verification: lint and build pass; 1173 unit (81 suites) and 1480 e2e (60 suites, 3 workers) pass. Not done: a manual Swagger click-through against Atlas.
Follow-up in the same PR (user: "fix them", about the known limit above): if RefundsService.issue fails after the status change, the reservation used to stay CANCELLED with no way to finish
the refund. Now the cancel route records what the cancellation refunds (two optional fields on the hotel booking schema: cancellationRequestedAt and cancellationRefundCents, written only while the
reservation is CONFIRMED, just before the status changes). A failure that is not a refusal by the refund rules answers 503 with a message saying the reservation is cancelled and to send the request again;
cancelling a CANCELLED reservation that has a recorded refund and no refund yet issues the recorded amount (not the tier of the retry day), also after the check-in time, and two simultaneous retries
give one refund. Tests first (7 new HTTP tests, Stripe failure simulated with a spy), watched failing; mutation check: issuing a different amount on retry failed three tests.
Verification of the follow-up: lint and build pass; 1192 unit (83 suites) and 1510 e2e (62 suites, 3 workers) pass. Not done: a manual Swagger click-through against Atlas.

word by word, right-aligned when the paragraph is right to left; brackets are mirrored. The app's own lines (time, name, cost) stay left to right, and a left-to-right mark (U+200E) after an Arabic activity name
keeps the cost out of the Arabic run (the algorithm otherwise splits a hyphenated number after Arabic letters). The test helper now decodes embedded fonts through their ToUnicode maps. package.json and the lockfile

Related Requirement: GEN-11 (SCRUM-33) Booking lifecycle: CONFIRMED to COMPLETED for flights
Prompt:
Verify GEN-11 against current develop for a scrum-master audit (GitHub issue #159): CONFIRMED to CANCELLED has callers in
both Flights and Hotels now, but nothing ever moves a CONFIRMED flight booking to COMPLETED. FlightStatusJob only stores a
Flight's own DEPARTED status, never touches a booking. (Hotels already has this: a daily @Cron sweep plus a lazy per-read
check in hotel-bookings.service.ts, both calling BookingLifecycle.transition; that half of the original audit was stale.)
Add the flights equivalent, with a test.
How the output was used:
Added completeArrivedBookings (a daily @Cron(EVERY_DAY_AT_MIDNIGHT) job) and completePastFlights to FlightBookingsService,
mirroring HotelBookingsService.completeCheckedOutStays/completePastStays exactly: same cursor-batched, idempotent,
exception-tolerant loop, same BookingLifecycle.transition(FLIGHT, id, COMPLETED) call. The one real difference is that a
flight booking's arrival time isn't stored on the booking itself (unlike a hotel reservation's checkOut), so "due"
bookings are found with an aggregation: $match CONFIRMED + cursor, then $addFields the last segment's flightId
($arrayElemAt(segments.flightId, -1), falling back to the booking's own top-level flightId for a legacy pre-FLT-12
booking with no segments array), $lookup into flights for its arrivalAt, then filter on arrivalAt <= now. The $limit is
placed after that arrival filter, not right after the initial $match, because most CONFIRMED bookings on any day are
future flights — limiting before filtering would make a short page falsely signal "nothing else due" while later,
overdue bookings are still waiting in the cursor.
Added a GEN-11 describe block to flight-bookings-hold.e2e-spec.ts calling FlightBookingsService.completePastFlights(now)
directly (same pattern hotel-bookings.e2e-spec.ts already uses for its own sweep), with flights and bookings inserted
directly into the test database so their arrival time can already be in the past (FLT-03 rejects a past departure
through the real endpoint). Four cases: a one-way booking completes once its flight has arrived; a booking is left
CONFIRMED while its flight has not arrived yet; a round trip completes only once its return leg (the last segment) has
arrived, proving the sweep doesn't complete early on the outbound alone; the sweep is idempotent (a second run completes
nothing further).

Related Requirement: GEN-01 (SCRUM-32) In-app notifications fired for every required event
Prompt:
Verify GEN-01 against current develop (63da298) for a scrum-master audit (GitHub issue #158): the requirement lists booking
confirmation, cancellation, refund, flight schedule change, and account/provider suspension as required notification events.
Confirmed confirmation, refund, schedule-change and suspension all already notify; the one real gap is cancellation —
BOOKING_CANCELLED is only sent when an airline cancels a flight (flight-cancellation.service.ts), never when a traveler
cancels their own flight or hotel booking. Those two paths only trigger a refund notification, and only when the refund is
positive, so a 0%-tier cancellation notifies nothing at all. Fix it in both cancel services and add a test.
How the output was used:
Added a notifyCancelled() call in FlightBookingsService.cancel() (FLT-26, right after BookingLifecycle.transition to
CANCELLED, before the refund is issued) and in HotelBookingCancellationService.cancel() (HTL-21), each injecting the
already-exported NotificationsService (both modules already import NotificationsModule for their other notify calls).
Each sends a distinct BOOKING_CANCELLED notification with a bookingReference-aware message and a /flight-bookings/{id} or
/hotel-bookings/{id} link, mirroring the exact message/link style flight-cancellation.service.ts already uses for the
provider-cancellation case. Not added to the already-CANCELLED retry-refund-completion path in either service, since that
would re-notify a cancellation that already happened. Added e2e assertions in flight-bookings-hold.e2e-spec.ts (the
positive-refund cancel test, asserting one BOOKING_CANCELLED plus the pre-existing REFUND_UPDATE, and that the already-
cancelled retry sends no second BOOKING_CANCELLED; the zero-refund cancel test, asserting the notification still fires
even though RefundsService never runs) and hotel-booking-cancel.e2e-spec.ts (same two cases), filtering on the booking's
own link rather than a raw count since travelerId is shared across the whole spec file. Added a notifications cleanup to
hotel-booking-cancel.e2e-spec.ts's beforeEach for the same reason (it wiped hotelBookings/payments/refunds but not
notifications).

# TASK: SCRUM-136 — GEN-05

## Project Information

Repository: Sanad-Manga/Travel-Planning-and-Booking-Platform

GitHub:
https://github.com/Sanad-Manga/Travel-Planning-and-Booking-Platform

Jira:
https://ahmedsanadprojects.atlassian.net/browse/SCRUM-136

Milestone: M2 — Frontend
Sprint: M2 S0 — Foundations
Owner: Mohamed Walid
Plan ID: M2-04
Requirement: GEN-05
Priority: Mandatory
Estimated effort: 4 hours

Task title:
[GEN-05] DataTable (responsive) + Pagination + usePaginated (page size 10)

Your responsibility is to implement, test, and verify this ticket completely while preserving existing project functionality and other contributors' work.

1. MILESTONE PROTECTION AND SCOPE POLICY

Milestone 1 contains the completed backend implementation.

Treat completed Milestone 1 functionality as protected, not permanently immutable.

Backend changes are permitted during Milestone 2 only when the assigned Jira ticket explicitly requires or authorizes them.

When backend changes are authorized in other tickets:
- Keep changes strictly within the approved ticket scope.
- Preserve backward compatibility wherever possible.
- Preserve existing API contracts unless an approved change requires otherwise.
- Avoid regressions.
- Run relevant existing and new regression tests.
- Document any changes affecting other contributors.

HOWEVER, SCRUM-136 IS A FRONTEND-ONLY TASK.

For this ticket:

- The entire backend/ directory is read-only.
- Existing API contracts are read-only.
- Existing Milestone 1 documentation is protected.
- Existing backend tests and database schemas are protected.
- Existing CI workflows and Git hooks are protected.
- Existing environment and deployment configuration is protected.
- Existing milestone tags and releases must remain untouched.

You may inspect these files to understand the project.

You must not modify them.

If you discover a backend bug, missing endpoint, incorrect response, Swagger inconsistency, security issue, or architectural problem:

1. Document the problem with supporting evidence.
2. Identify the affected files or API endpoints.
3. Explain whether it prevents completion of SCRUM-136.
4. Recommend a separately scoped Jira fix where appropriate.
5. Do not implement that fix without explicit authorization.

Do not modify the backend simply to make frontend implementation easier.

Do not fabricate API responses, silently hide backend problems, or weaken validation to make incompatible behavior appear functional.

Preserve existing frontend functionality as well.

Do not implement:

- SCRUM-137.
- SCRUM-141.
- Downstream admin pages.
- Hotel moderator pages.
- Unrelated frontend features.
- Other contributors' Jira tickets.

If any requirement conflicts with these restrictions, preserve the restrictions and report the blocker.

2. GIT SETUP AND REPOSITORY SAFETY

Complete this section before making implementation changes.

A. Inspect the local repository

Run:

5. OBJECTIVE AND DELIVERABLES

Implement reusable frontend pagination infrastructure.

2. Reusable Pagination component.
3. Reusable usePaginated hook.

5. Concise integration documentation and usage examples.

6. DATATABLE COMPONENT

Suggested location:

frontend/src/components/DataTable.tsx

Follow existing repository conventions if another location is more appropriate.

Create a generic DataTable<T> component.

FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS

- Accept typed row data.
- Accept typed column definitions.
- Support stable column IDs.
- Support column headers.
- Support custom cell-rendering functions.
- Support React content inside cells.
- Support stable row keys supplied by consumers.
- Support empty data.
- Support loading states.
- Support error states.
- Work with arbitrary business-domain row types.

Avoid using array indexes as the default identity for dynamic rows.

ACCESSIBILITY

Use semantic HTML:

- table
- thead
- tbody
- tr
- th
- td

Provide:

- Appropriate column header scope.
- Accessible table name or caption.
- Valid table structure.
- Keyboard-accessible interactive cell content.
- Visible focus indicators.

Do not use color alone to communicate important information.

RESPONSIVENESS

- Work on mobile, tablet, and desktop.
- Handle wide tables using an appropriately scoped overflow container.
- Prevent horizontal overflow of the entire page.
- Preserve semantic table markup.
- Support long cell content and action buttons.

Do not replace the table with inaccessible div-based layouts.

STATES

Reuse existing components where appropriate:

- EmptyState.
- Skeleton.
- Spinner.
- Alert.
- Existing friendly error formatting.

Distinguish between:

- Initial loading.
- Successful results.
- Successful empty results.
- Failed requests.

Do not show an empty state while data is still loading.

Do not present failed requests as successful empty results.

OUT OF SCOPE

Do not implement:

- Spreadsheet editing.
- Column dragging.
- Advanced virtualization.
- Custom sorting engines.
- CSV export.
- Domain-specific filters.
- Domain-specific business logic.

Keep the component API small, typed, and documented.

7. PAGINATION COMPONENT

Suggested location:

frontend/src/components/Pagination.tsx

Create a reusable controlled Pagination component.

It must not fetch API data itself.

FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS

- Use page size 10 by default.
- Display the current page.
- Display the total number of pages.
- Display useful result counts when available.
- Provide Previous and Next controls.
- Disable Previous on the first page.
- Disable Next on the last page.
- Handle a single page.
- Handle zero results.
- Handle partially filled final pages.
- Prevent invalid navigation.
- Invoke the consumer's navigation callback.
- Work on mobile and desktop.

Never display:

- Page 0.
- Page 1 of 0.
- Negative page numbers.
- Misleading result counts.

Use server-provided pagination metadata.

Do not calculate the total number of pages using only the current items array.

Do not introduce internal pagination state that conflicts with the consumer's state.

ACCESSIBILITY

- Use an accessible navigation landmark.
- Give buttons descriptive accessible names.
- Use proper disabled states.
- Support keyboard interaction.
- Preserve visible focus indicators.
- Clearly communicate the current page.

Prefer a simple Previous / page information / Next layout unless existing project requirements specify otherwise.

8. usePaginated HOOK

Suggested location:

frontend/src/api/usePaginated.ts

Implement a generic reusable hook for server-side paginated data.

EXISTING UTILITIES

Reuse:

- useApi<T> from frontend/src/api/useApi.ts
- Page<T> from frontend/src/api/types.ts
- withQuery() from frontend/src/api/types.ts
- Existing SWRProvider
- Existing centralized API client

Do not create another API client.

Do not duplicate authentication handling.

Do not bypass existing SWR configuration.

BACKEND CONTRACT

Query parameters:

page:
- Default: 1.

limit:
- Default: 10.
- Maximum: 50.

Existing response structure:

{
  "items": [],
  "page": 1,
  "limit": 10,
  "total": 0,
  "totalPages": 0
}

Reuse the existing Page<T> interface.

Do not redefine or change the backend response structure.

FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS

The hook must:

- Start on page 1.
- Use a default limit of 10.
- Manage selected-page state.
- Expose the current page's items.
- Expose pagination metadata.
- Expose loading state.
- Expose error state.
- Expose navigation functions or a page setter.
- Expose current-page revalidation through the existing SWR mechanism.
- Support arbitrary paginated endpoints.
- Support additional query filters.
- Support disabling a request when its endpoint is unavailable.
- Generate distinct request keys for different endpoints, pages, and filters.
- Reset pagination when the endpoint or meaningful filters change.
- Prevent filters from accidentally overriding page and limit.
- Prevent navigation below page 1.
- Prevent navigation beyond known valid page boundaries.
- Handle empty results.
- Handle API errors.
- Handle datasets that shrink after deletion or revalidation.
- Preserve existing authentication and error behavior.

If a configurable page size is supported, validate it against the existing backend contract.

Do not introduce a page-size selection UI unless explicitly required.

FILTER AND CACHE BEHAVIOR

- Use stable, deterministic SWR request keys.
- Equivalent filter objects must not reset pagination merely because their references differ.
- A filter change must not produce an unintended request for the previous filter's page number.
- Prevent stale responses from replacing the currently selected page's results.
- Do not display old page metadata as if it belongs to the new page.
- Distinguish initial loading from background revalidation where appropriate.
- Avoid unnecessary API requests and rendering loops.

Handle page shrinkage safely.

For example, if the user is on page 4 and a deletion reduces the total number of pages to 3, the hook should recover to a valid page without an infinite request loop.

If totalPages becomes 0, maintain a valid internal page selection of 1 while displaying an appropriate empty state.

Do not double-paginate server-paginated responses.

TYPE SAFETY

- Use generic TypeScript types.
- Reuse generated API types where applicable.
- Reuse Page<T>.
- Avoid any.
- Avoid unnecessary type assertions.
- Do not suppress compiler errors.
- Never manually edit frontend/src/api/schema.d.ts.

Keep the hook understandable and maintainable for downstream contributors.

9. DESIGN AND INTEGRATION

Follow the current frontend design system.

Reuse:

- Existing CSS variables.
- Existing colors.
- Existing typography.
- Existing spacing.
- Existing button styles.
- Existing dark-mode behavior.
- Existing error and loading components.

If additional CSS is needed:

- Prefer minimal additive styling.

No new UI frameworks or large third-party libraries.

Provide a concise usage example showing:

1. Calling usePaginated with a paginated endpoint.
2. Defining typed columns.
3. Passing returned rows to DataTable.

5. Connecting Pagination to hook state.

The three deliverables must work together without requiring backend changes.

Use test fixtures and documentation for examples.

GEN-08:

The system shall accept image uploads in JPG or PNG format, up to 5 MB each, with up to 5 images per post, activity or hotel and 1 logo per airline, stored on the server and served by URL.

Uploads outside these limits are rejected with an error.

Component:

ImageUploader

Images are served from /uploads/... on the backend, outside /api.

- Shows previews and a delete button.
- Shows the backend error text on failure.
- Works with the keyboard and on phones.

5. IMAGE UPLOADER COMPONENT
==================================================

Suggested location:

frontend/src/components/ImageUploader.tsx

Create a reusable, strictly typed ImageUploader component.

It must be suitable for:

- Blog post images.
- Itinerary activity images.
- Hotel photos.
- Airline logos.

Do not hardcode the component to any individual entity or API endpoint.

Use a configurable interface that accepts existing image references, upload and delete operations, and the appropriate image-count limit.

Prefer a controlled component or a clearly documented equivalent that integrates with downstream forms.

An image reference generally contains:

{
  "id": "server-generated-id",
  "url": "/uploads/example.jpg"
}

Confirm actual response shapes and allow consumers to adapt them where necessary.

Support:

- Rendering existing uploaded images.
- Selecting new images.
- Uploading through the provided operation.
- Displaying image previews.
- Deleting individual images.
- Uploading and deleting states.
- Validation errors.
- Backend errors.
- Disabled controls while relevant requests are pending.
- Updating the consumer with successfully uploaded or deleted images.

Do not claim an image has been uploaded or deleted before the server operation succeeds.

On deletion failure, retain the image and display the error.

On upload failure, do not create a fake successful image reference.

Keep the component API small and predictable.

==================================================
6. FILE VALIDATION
==================================================

Match the existing backend limits.

Accepted MIME types:

image/jpeg
image/png

Maximum image size:

5,242,880 bytes (5 MiB)

Maximum images:

- Post: 5.
- Activity: 5.
- Hotel: 5.
- Airline: 1.

The default limit should be 5.

Support a maximum of 1 for airline logos.

Validate files BEFORE sending any upload request.

Reject:

- Unsupported MIME types.
- Oversized files.
- Empty files.
- Files exceeding the remaining available image slots.

Include the existing images and relevant pending uploads when checking capacity.

Accept valid images exactly at the maximum size.

Provide clear messages explaining why a file was rejected.

The file input accept attribute should advertise JPEG and PNG formats, but actual validation must still be performed.

Do not trust file extensions alone.

The backend remains responsible for authoritative content validation, decoding, ownership, authorization, and atomic capacity enforcement.

Do not duplicate the backend image-decoding implementation in the browser.

Prevent accidental duplicate submissions.

When selecting multiple files, respect the remaining capacity. Keep upload behavior predictable and document whether selections are processed sequentially or rejected as a batch.

==================================================
7. UPLOAD AND DELETE API INTEGRATION
==================================================

The backend does NOT provide one universal POST /api/uploads endpoint.

Existing consumer-owned endpoints include:

Blog:
POST /api/posts/:id/images
DELETE /api/posts/:id/images/:imageId

Hotel:
POST /api/hotels/:id/photos
DELETE /api/hotels/:id/photos/:photoId

Airline:
POST /api/airlines/:id/logo
DELETE /api/airlines/:id/logo

Activity:
Inspect the current backend controller to confirm the exact endpoint paths and response shapes.

Existing upload operations use multipart/form-data. Blog and airline endpoints use the multipart field "image"; verify the other endpoints before documenting their usage.

Use FormData for multipart requests.

Do NOT manually set the multipart Content-Type header because the browser must generate the boundary.

Important discovery:

frontend/src/lib/api.ts currently serializes request bodies as JSON.

If necessary, extend the existing centralized API client minimally to support FormData.

Requirements for that extension:

- Existing JSON requests must behave exactly as before.
- FormData must be transmitted without JSON.stringify().
- Do not set application/json for FormData requests.
- Preserve same-origin routing.
- Preserve credentials: include.
- Preserve existing 401 refresh and retry behavior.
- Preserve ApiError parsing.
- Preserve existing login redirects.
- Do not introduce a duplicate authentication implementation.
- Do not automatically retry failed uploads for arbitrary network or server errors.
- Add targeted regression tests.

Use a reusable API integration pattern with configurable upload/delete operations.

Do not implement the downstream pages or all their domain-specific API modules merely to demonstrate the shared component.

Preserve the server's authorization model.

Do not expose authentication tokens to JavaScript or localStorage.

3. ACTUAL JIRA REQUIREMENTS
==================================================

What to build:

Small backend fix. Admin needs to pick an airline or hotel when creating a moderator or deactivating a provider, but no endpoint lists them. Add a list endpoint for admin only.

ACC-13:

The system shall allow an admin to create a hotel or airline moderator account (name, email, role) linked to exactly one hotel or airline, with a temporary password set by the admin. The moderator must change the password on first login.

ACC-15:

The system shall allow an admin to suspend and reactivate a moderator account and, separately, deactivate and reactivate a hotel or airline.

A deactivated provider's listings are hidden from search and cannot be booked. Existing Confirmed bookings remain valid. Affected parties receive an in-app notification.

IMPORTANT:

These requirements explain why the provider list is necessary.

Do not reimplement ACC-13 or ACC-15.

Their existing functionality must remain unchanged.

New endpoint:

GET /api/admin/providers?type=airline
GET /api/admin/providers?type=hotel

The repository uses the global /api prefix, so the controller route should follow the existing admin route conventions.

Return:

- Provider ID.
- Provider name.
- Active state.

Admin only.

Document the endpoint in Swagger.

Acceptance criteria:

- Returns airlines or hotels by type for an admin.
- Other authenticated roles receive 403.
- Documented in Swagger.

5. ENDPOINT IMPLEMENTATION
==================================================

Implement:

GET /api/admin/providers

Required query parameter:

type=airline|hotel

Use the existing controller, DTO, and service conventions.

Choose the smallest clean implementation within the admin module.

A separate narrowly scoped controller/service is acceptable if it avoids modifying the existing provider status logic.

Do not create an entirely new NestJS module.

FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS

For type=airline:

Return airline providers only.

For type=hotel:

Return hotel providers only.

Return both active and inactive providers.

Inactive providers must remain available to admins so they can be reactivated.

Each provider item should contain only:

{
  "id": "provider-id",
  "name": "Provider name",
  "isActive": true
}

Confirm field names against existing project conventions.

Use string IDs consistent with existing API DTOs.

Do not return raw MongoDB documents.

Do not expose internal schema fields, private metadata, moderator credentials, payment information, or unrelated provider data.

Use explicit database projection and response mapping.

Use deterministic sorting consistent with existing list endpoints, such as name followed by ID, or stable ascending ID order.

The endpoint must be read-only.

It must never:

- Create a provider.
- Update a provider.
- Change provider status.
- Create a moderator.
- Change account status.
- Modify a booking.
- Send notifications.
- Trigger existing mutation logic.

Do not add unrelated filters or features.

- Maximum limit 50 works.

C. Authorization

- ADMIN receives 200.
- USER receives 403.
- AIRLINE_MODERATOR receives 403.
- HOTEL_MODERATOR receives 403.
- Unauthenticated requests receive 401.

How the output was used:
Added api/blog.ts (listPosts, listPostsByTag, key builders, post and tag links), features/blog (PostList cards with author, date, excerpt, clickable tags and like/comment counts; BlogSearchForm, a plain GET form so search works without JavaScript; PagerLinks, the shared Pagination driving the URL so the server renders each page; BlogResults with list, empty, past-the-last-page and error states; loadPosts, which turns backend failures into a friendly Alert and a rejected tag into the 404 page; a CSS module using only theme tokens) and the two route files with generateMetadata (title naming the search or tag, description, Open Graph, canonical; search result pages set noindex). Tags are lower-cased and trimmed like the backend, a tag over 30 characters is a 404 without calling the backend, and page numbers that are not whole numbers from 1 up fall back to page 1. Removed /blog from the GEN-14 placeholder-page test because it is no longer a placeholder. emptyMessages.posts links to /posts/new, so the blog page overrides that action with /blog/new.
Verification: npm run lint, npm run typecheck and npm run build clean (both routes dynamic); npx vitest run 649/649 passed (15 new tests in two files). Ran it against the real local backend at 375 px with throwaway data (a test traveller and 12 published posts plus 1 draft, all marked "[SCRUM-193 test]", deleted afterwards and the account deactivated): the list showed 10 cards and "Page 1 of 2", Next went to /blog?page=2 with the last 2 posts, the draft never appeared, the search form found "koshary" only, a search by author name and a mixed search + tag worked, ?tag=FOOD matched lower-case, /blog/tag/cairo and /blog/tag/road%20trip listed the right posts, ?page=7 showed the past-the-end message, a 31-character tag gave 404, an unmatched search showed the search empty state, and there was no horizontal scroll. With the backend stopped, the error alert was readable.
